Latin Josephus Project

Manuscripts of the Latin Josephus

Pollard & Aubé-Pronce

Richard Matthew Pollard & Jean-Félix Aubé-Pronce

An original contribution by Pollard & Aubé-Pronce, bringing together manuscripts, fragments and excerpts not assembled in any single earlier list, with links to online images and catalogues where available.

De excidio Hierosolymitano (DEH, conventionally Pseudo-Hegesippus) is included because it was attributed to Josephus in manuscripts up to the mid-ninth century, and sometimes beyond. Pollard 2015.

Version of 29 October 2026 · 511 manuscript records · Five related epitome witnesses

Sigla and family classifications incorporate Ussani, Blatt, Levenson and Martin (L-M) (2021 revision), Bader and Lukas. Each authority is named; family assignments apply to the work or passage indicated. The principal previous list of DEH manuscripts is in the CD-ROM addenda to Lanéry’s Ambroise de Milan hagiographe (2008) (lists 131); see also the DEH manuscript list in Bay (2023) (lists 78). Full citations appear in the bibliography.

AJ = Antiquitates Judaicae; BJ = the seven-book Latin Bellum Judaicum; CA = Contra Apionem; DEH = the five-book De excidio Hierosolymitano. Historical and uncertain witnesses are marked; records are not a count of surviving codices.

Images = manuscript images; Selected images = selected folios or bindings; † = live access unconfirmed; — = no information recorded. A missing link does not establish that no digitisation exists. Links checked in October 2026.

511 manuscript records; five related epitome witnesses

Notes and references

KU Leuven Limo, lbsn9992384417401471, lists AJ XV–XX on ff. 1r–61v and BJ on ff. 62v–158v, and dates the manuscript to 1401–1500. This differs from the MMFC text dating retained below. Physical description: 158 folios, 420 × 299 mm, vellum, two columns and 50 lines. Companion volume: PM0037/V°.

Tentative grouping suggested in correspondence supplied with the Limo references: grH or grG (unverified; classification scheme and work scope unconfirmed).

Formerly Mechelen, Bibliotheek Grootseminarie, MS 3. The current MMFC catalogue dates the text to the sixteenth century and the binding to the fifteenth; the earlier fifteenth-century dating is retained here with this qualification.

Other shelfmarks or catalogue numbers: Mechelen, Bibliothèque du Grand Séminaire, ms. 3.

  • Blatt 1958, pp. 26–94, no. 137; AJ: ml. One siglum covers the paired or divided volumes listed together in the source. Former Mechelen MSS 2 and 3, now Leuven PM0037/V° and PM0021/V°.
  • Blatt 1958, pp. 26–94, no. 137; AJ: φ. Antiquities classification; do not transfer this family to BJ, CA or DEH in the same volume.

Bibliography

Sources of sigla and family classifications:

  • Ussani, Vincenzo, ed. Hegesippi qui dicitur historiae libri V. Pars prior: Textum criticum continens. Corpus Scriptorum Ecclesiasticorum Latinorum 66.1. Vienna: Hoelder-Pichler-Tempsky; Leipzig: Akademische Verlagsgesellschaft, 1932.
  • Blatt, Franz, ed. The Latin Josephus. I: Introduction and Text. The Antiquities: Books I–V. Acta Jutlandica 30.1, Humanistisk Serie 44. Aarhus: Universitetsforlaget i Aarhus; Copenhagen: Ejnar Munksgaard, 1958.
  • Levenson, David B., and Thomas R. Martin. “The Ancient Latin Translations of Josephus.” In A Companion to Josephus, edited by Honora Howell Chapman and Zuleika Rodgers, 322–344. Chichester: Wiley-Blackwell, 2016.
  • Levenson, David B., and Thomas R. Martin. “A Revised Classification of Manuscript Groups for the Early Books of the Latin Translation of Josephus’s Antiquities Based on Textual Variants in AJ 6.356–360 and 6.362b.” Medievalia et Humanistica, n.s., 46 (2021): 71–148.
  • Bader, Bernd, ed. Josephus Latinus: De bello Iudaico. Buch 1. Herausgegeben und kommentiert von Bernd Bader. Palingenesia 119. Stuttgart: Franz Steiner Verlag, 2019.
  • Lukas, Randolf. Josephus Latinus, Antiquitates Judaicae. Buch 6 und 7. Einleitung, Edition und Kommentar zur Übersetzungstechnik. Bochumer Altertumswissenschaftliches Colloquium 112. Trier: Wissenschaftlicher Verlag Trier, 2022.

For DEH, the Gießen fragments and the related epitome:

  • Lanéry, Cécile. Ambroise de Milan hagiographe. Collection des Études Augustiniennes, Série Antiquité 183. Paris: Institut d’Études Augustiniennes, 2008. Addenda on the accompanying CD-ROM (DEH manuscript list).
  • Bay, Carson. Biblical Heroes and Classical Culture in Christian Late Antiquity: The Historiography, Exemplarity, and Anti-Judaism of Pseudo-Hegesippus. Cambridge: Cambridge University Press, 2023. DEH manuscript list. Publisher’s edition.

  • Bader, Bernd. Fragmenta Gissensia: Antike lateinische Literatur in Handschriftenfragmenten der UB Gießen. Berichte und Arbeiten aus der Universitätsbibliothek und dem Universitätsarchiv Gießen 63. Gießen: Universitätsbibliothek, 2015.
  • Linder, Amnon. “Ps. Ambrose’s Anacephalaeosis: A Carolingian Treatise on the Destruction of Jerusalem.” Revue d’histoire des textes 22 (1992): 145–158.
